## Day 19: The Perfect Funnel

<!-- Source: Day 19 - The Perfect Funnel.pdf; page 1 -->

**ACQUISITION → CONVERSION → MONETIZATION**

START → PAID / ORGANIC → LEADS → BUYERS → BEST BUYERS

PAID also leads directly to BUYERS.

| LEAD MAGNET | FRONT-END PRODUCT | BACK-END PRODUCT 1 | BACK-END PRODUCT 2 | | --- | --- | --- | --- | | Teaser | Abundance | Certainty | Fanatism | | Free | Cheap | Automated | High-ticket | | | Recurring (optional) | | Recurring (optional) |

Most Copywriters stick to writing emails, sales letters, and VSLs...

...Which is why they get stuck at the ‘good money’ level, and never reach the ‘great money’ level.

But you’re not like most Copywriters.

If you want to charge higher fees and provide upsells to your clients, you must know the architecture of a winning business.

Let’s say you’re driving a car. No matter how great it is, it won’t get you across the river if you don’t have a bridge to drive onto.

Well, systems are this bridge. And without a strong system, all Copy will flop, no matter how well it’s written.

So if you cannot analyze the system, you’ll be left wondering why your Copy falls flat and you’ll be doubting yourself!

That’s why today I’m going to show you a strong system you will be able to understand and copy...

I call it ‘The Perfect Funnel.’

And I’m not just talking about a product funnel here... I’m talking about a larger funnel at the scope of the business.

This is the exact method the best Direct Response companies use to structure their business. And for a very simple reason...


<!-- Source: Day 19 - The Perfect Funnel.pdf; page 2 -->

...This architecture works.

I have searched for years on end to find a better one, and I’m yet to find it.

Before we get on with it, here’s a quick disclaimer...

This is a dense topic. Do not expect to remember this lesson - or even understand it - straight away. See it more like a hands-on guide you can refer to anytime.

With practice however it’ll become second nature.

So without further ado, here is The Perfect Funnel...

You start by separating the business into 3.

1. Acquisition = getting new leads, or customers
2. Conversion = turning leads into first time customers
3. Monetization = selling multiple times, to maximize revenue

Each of these 3 steps have their own ‘Why,’ their own logic.

Non-buyer, Buyer and Best Buyer are the only segments you need.

Anything more precise becomes tedious.

To illustrate The Perfect Funnel, I’ll be using a public speaking coaching business.

At the end of the lesson, on page 16, you’ll find another example that uses physical products.

Now let’s see the first pillar of The Perfect Funnel...

Acquisition

The goal of Acquisition is straightforward: How can you get new customers in a way that’s sustainable and predictable?

Ideally, you want to make a profit or at least break even right from the start.

Think of it this way: If you spend $100 to get a customer who immediately brings you $120...

You can keep doing it forever.


<!-- Source: Day 19 - The Perfect Funnel.pdf; page 3 -->

And that's what we're aiming for: measurable and scalable.

There are 2 ways to acquire customers:

1. Paid acquisition: Ads, affiliates, native advertising
2. Organic acquisition: Content, SEO, social media

And no matter your business or niche, you should eventually be doing both at the same time.

However, if you’re tight on time, but not on money, focus on paid.

And if you’re tight on money, but not on time, focus on organic.

Paid acquisition is done through:

- Ads: Meta, Google, Tik Tok...
- Natives: Taboola, Outbrain...
- Affiliates: Clickbank, Digistore24...

The key question is: How much can you spend to acquire ONE customer and still be profitable?

You want to find the most viable strategy long term, where the ‘Lifetime Value’ of one customer is greater than the cost of your acquisition...

SO you’re left with actual profit.

It’s the reason why we have ‘Conversion’ and ‘Monetization’ down the road.

But for now let’s zoom in on a great paid acquisition system, and see how it works...

This system allows you to be at break-even from the get-go, and even profitable in some cases.

It has 5 components:

1.A low-ticket product: <$100 for most niches 2.A first upsell: up to 10-20x the low ticket product
3. A downsell, in case they don’t buy the upsell 4.A cross sell
5. A downsell to your cross sell


<!-- Source: Day 19 - The Perfect Funnel.pdf; page 4 -->

Quick note: A cross sell is when you offer a related, but different, product to a customer who has just purchased.

Think ‘fries with your burger.’

A downsell is when you offer a cheaper alternative to someone who has declined your main offer.

It’s like offering a basic, no frills, mobile plan after someone declined the premium plan with unlimited 5G.

Don’t worry: We’ll be covering both in detail in a few days.

Now back to the paid acquisition system...

Here’s how you know whether your system for acquiring a client is profitable

The equation looks like this:

PROFIT PER CUSTOMER = CAC − [PRICE OF THE PRODUCT] − [AOV OF THE UPSELL, DOWNSELLS & CROSS SELLS]

Here’s a breakdown:

CAC = Customer Acquisition Cost, that is, how much acquiring a new customer costs you.

Price Of The Product = The price of your main product.

AOV Of The Upsell, Downsells & Cross sells = The ‘Average Order Value’ of how much a customer spends on your upsell, downsells & cross sells

Now let’s illustrate it with our public speaking coaching.

I know numbers can get confusing, so we’ll take it step by step.

Here’s what we’ll be working with...

Imagine you've set up a funnel with these products:

- A low-ticket offer at $6 (your entry point)
- “Spellbound: The 60-Page Guide To Captivating Your Audience”


<!-- Source: Day 19 - The Perfect Funnel.pdf; page 5 -->

- An upsell at $97 (your premium offer) © “Stage Ready: The Complete Video Course On How To Prepare For & Deliver A Presentation That Leaves Your Audience Asking For More”

- A downsell at $37 (for those who say no to the upsell) © “Stage Ready Essential Video Modules: The 4 Crucial Elements Of A Captivating Presentation”

- A cross-sell at $199 (your related product) © “Voice Mastery Workshop: 1-Hour Recording”

- And a monthly downsell at $39 (a cheaper version of the cross-sell) © “Voice Mastery Foundations”

Now imagine you spent $1,000 on Facebook ads and got 50 people to buy your low-ticket offer...

$1,000 ÷ 50 = $20

You’ve spent $20 to acquire one customer. This is your CAC.

All 50 people have bought your $6 product.

50 x 6 = $300

You got $300 in revenue.

But here’s where it gets interesting with this funnel...

Not everyone will stop at the first purchase!

20% of those 50 people think ‘Yes, I want more!’ when they see your upsell sales page.

That’s 10 x $97 = $970 in added revenue

So you’re sitting at $1,290 in total.

The people who’ve passed on your upsell are shown the sales page to your downsell.

10% of those 50 buyers see it and think ‘Well, $37 sounds more suitable to my budget,’ so they buy your downsell.

There’s another 5 x $37 = $185 in added revenue


<!-- Source: Day 19 - The Perfect Funnel.pdf; page 6 -->

You’re up to $1,475 in total.

But we’re not done yet.

5% of the 50 people love what you’re offering and buy your $199 cross sell. ‘2.5 people’ doesn’t exist so we round it down to 2.

That’s 2 x $199 = $398 in added revenue

Which brings you up to $1,873 in total.

Finally, 2 more people (another 5% of the 50 buyers) grab your monthly downsell at $39, adding $78 to your total revenue.

Which ends up being $1,951.

We can now calculate your Average Order Value of all those add-ons.

We start by adding up all the revenue the upsell, downsells and cross sells have brought in, like so:

$970 + $185 + $398 + $78 = $1,631

And we divide it by the total number of buyers, which is 50:

$1,631 ÷ 50 = $32.62

Each buyer has spent an average of $32.62 on your upsell, downsells and cross sells.

In reality, most have spent the minimum $6 while only a handful have spent a maximum of $302 ($6 + $97 + $199).

Now, let’s see how much profit you’ve made per customer...

We start with our CAC: -$20 (‘minus’ because you’ve spent it)

We add your product: +$6

We add your average additional revenue: +$32.62

And we’re left with our profit per customer = $18.62

Each customer has brought us $18.62 in profit.

Now let’s look at the overall campaign numbers.


<!-- Source: Day 19 - The Perfect Funnel.pdf; page 7 -->

You’ve spent $1,000 on ads and made $1,931 in revenue.

So you’re left with $931 in net profit.

Which means that for every $1 spent, you get $1,93 back, out of which $0,93 is pure profit.

Quick disclaimer...

These numbers are achievable, but quite optimistic.

And it’s much more complicated than that in real life, especially for physical products because you’ve got to take into account things like the costs for delivering your products, taxes, fees, payment processing costs...

But that’s not the point of this example.

The point is to show you that YOU CAN break even and even be profitable with a good acquisition offer...

..WHILE having a cheap enough product that gets as many people in the door as possible, which is your #1 goal at this stage.

Because the real profit comes from what happens next, once they’ve spent that first bit of money on one of your products.

Now here’s what most marketers get wrong...

They think that the only way to increase the profit per customer is to increase the price of their product.

But this only decreases your conversion rate, which kills your revenue in the long run.

In simple terms, a lower conversion rate on your front-end offer means less first-time customers, which means less opportunity to sell them your back-end offers, and less revenue in the long run.

As surprising as it may seem, there’s a MASSIVE difference in profitability between a 7% conversion rate and 10%.

The real needle-movers are the other 2 components of the equation:

1. Your CAC: You can lower it by either lowering your costs for acquiring customers making your sales page convert better. The latter is a better option, but harder to do.


<!-- Source: Day 19 - The Perfect Funnel.pdf; page 8 -->

2. Your AOV of the upsell, downsell & cross sell: You can increase how much each customer spends by improving those products and their sales pages

Quick note: a lower price on the front-end isn’t necessarily better, because you still want to attract the right kind of customers.

In any case, you want to have a deep understanding of the math for your funnel.

It helps you see exactly where the money is coming from and where you can improve.

Now let’s go over the second pillar of The Perfect Funnel...

Conversion

Conversion is the most important component of any business that acquires leads, which should be any business.

Yes, even ‘high-ticket’ coaching businesses...

Because there’s no such thing as a ‘high-ticket-only’ business.

Does it exist? Yes, but it’s hard to scale. You can do high-ticket acquisition, but never alone by itself.

Remember this: A winning business has multiple acquisition channels running at the same, both free and paid.

Most marketers don’t think about varying their acquisition strategies enough. But in the end, it’s all about math and probabilities. Having only one drastically reduces your reach.

Understand this: Lead generation, whether paid or organic, is crucial. And you can do it through a lead magnet.

A lead magnet is a free product that acts as a teaser to your first paid offer.

It should solve a problem AND create another problem which your low-ticket product solves in turn.

For instance:

- Lead magnet: “The 5-Min Confidence Boost: Overcome Public Speaking Anxiety With Short Pre-Speech Ritual”
- Low Ticket Product: “Spellbound: The 60-Page Guide To Captivating Your Audience”


<!-- Source: Day 19 - The Perfect Funnel.pdf; page 9 -->

The keyword here is: ‘Teaser.’

You want to attract as many qualified leads as possible, to then turn them into first-time buyers.

So no giveaways or sweepstakes... This will NOT attract a qualified audience.

Once your lead has downloaded your lead magnet, you can sell them your low-ticket offer.

It can be a book, a mini-course, a special report, a ‘free-with-shipping’ offer, a sample, etc.

You just need to make sure it’s consistent with what precedes and what follows. It’s a continuum that needs to make sense from a bird’s-eye view.

Also keep in mind the goal of Conversion: You want people to take out their credit card for the first time.

That’s why the keyword of Conversion is ‘Abundance.’

You must make them an ABSURD offer they would feel stupid to pass on...

But also an offer that’s easy to digest and follow.

Because you want them to consume the content, not just buy it.

So don’t send them 1,000 pages and 25 hours of content, because they’ll never consume everything.

Plus, they will see this as work, and will lose interest in your business.

On the other hand you can send them something valuable AND quick to consume...

..Like an ebook that’s less than 100 pages...

+ Bonus 1: 2 or 3 10-minute bonus video lessons... + Bonus 2: A 1-hour bonus workshop... + Bonus 3: A special exercise worksheet...

Back to our example for the public speaking coach, it could look like this:

“Spellbound: The 60-Page Guide To Captivating Your Audience”

+ Bonus 1: “The Full 10-Min Pre-Speech Ritual: A Follow-Along Video That Includes Face-Muscles Warm-Up, ‘Power Poses’ And Visualization”


<!-- Source: Day 19 - The Perfect Funnel.pdf; page 10 -->

+ Bonus 2: “The 15 Most Common Speech Mistakes Executives Make, A 1-hour excerpt from a private talk I made to the Ubisoft UK branch in 2023” + Bonus 3: “5 Speech Structure Templates Used By Y Combinator Founders”

Agora and the other big publishing companies usually offer a paid special report, or a low-ticket annual subscription to a magazine, to which they add plenty of bonuses like short books, stock picks, or video recordings of a seminar.

This is your FRONT-END offer...

.And its sole purpose is to convert, not to profit!

We generate most of our revenue later down the line, with Monetization.

Monetization

Monetization is everything that comes after the first sale.

This is the fun part!

It’s where you actually pay yourself back, and more.

The keyword of Monetization is ‘certainty’.

People will buy your back-end products not because you’ve made them a good promise like you did in your Conversion phase, when the keyword was ‘abundance...’

..But because you’ve made them a CREDIBLE promise backed up by proof.

Monetization comes in 2 forms:

- Selling a second or third front-end product
- Selling a higher priced back-end product

In the first case, we’re looking at a small bump in revenue.

In the second case you will not only cover your CAC, but also generate profit.

Back-end products are easier to sell in general because trust is high. With that said, they can also flop if you do not focus on ‘certainty.’

This is because you will be talking to a different type of person.


<!-- Source: Day 19 - The Perfect Funnel.pdf; page 11 -->

You’ve got 3 types of people in your funnel by now:
1. ‘Non-buyers:’ These people have been added to your list after downloading a lead magnet. Most of them are opportunists looking for the next shiny object - a.k.a. ‘leeches.’ And the vast majority will never buy anything from you.

Quick note: You also have what I call ‘Level 0 Buyers.’

These guys have bought your front-end product but they won’t engage with it. Maybe it was an impulse or maybe it was FOMO, in any case they’re bargain hunters and opportunity seekers. They often collect products without using them.

To make it simpler, I am including them here, but you don’t want to segment them. I just want you to be aware of them.

They’re like the guys who get a 3-month gym subscription in January and never step foot in it again. They’ve made a purchase, but they’re not committed to the process yet.

2. ‘Buyers:’ These customers have moved beyond the initial purchase and have purchased your first back-end offer. They’re action takers, they actually implement what they learn and they expect quality. They’re willing to invest more money for better results and they trust you to help them.

These are like gym members who, after trying some classes, sign up for personal training. They're ready to take things seriously.

3. ‘Best buyers:’ These guys have purchased 2 or more back-end offers from you. They fall into 2 categories:

a. The ‘Fanatics’ love everything about you and your brand, and it’s the main reason they buy.. Because it’s you! They don’t necessarily consume the products they buy, but that’s ok, because they’ re emotionally invested in your success. These guys are great for testimonials and social proof. They often become brand advocates.


<!-- Source: Day 19 - The Perfect Funnel.pdf; page 12 -->

b. The ‘Pure Doers’ buy mostly because they see the value in your offer. They implement everything they buy which is why they get consistent results... Which often makes them your best case studies!

Now that you know ‘who’s who,’ the idea is simple.

With your front-end product, be the next shiny object:

- Focus on excitement and opportunity
- Make irresistible offers
- Emphasize quick wins
- Use scarcity and urgency
- Keep it simple and compelling

With your back-end product, provide an actual transformation:

- Emphasize tangible results
- Focus on exclusivity
- Offer connection, community, special access, or privilege
- Use proof and case studies
- Build on previous successes

1-1 coaching and masterminds can be great back-end products and services, and they’re also the most effective at bringing actual results to customers.

Now what about subscription products? They can be relevant both on the front-end and the back-end.

But remember this: front-end subscriptions are harder to sell and shouldn’t be there to generate revenue. They simply allow you to pay back your CAC, allowing for higher and quicker ROI. One front-end subscriber will pay for multiple other buyers, which is the greatest thing about the model.

But you will not get rich with it alone! You still need strong (and pricey) back-end products.

On the back-end however, subscriptions are a great way to secure your financial situation. They can make for a comfortable recurring revenue stream, and the average back-end subscriber will stay with you longer.

For our public speaking coach, her back-end suite of products could look like this...


<!-- Source: Day 19 - The Perfect Funnel.pdf; page 13 -->

e® Back-end product 1: "Presentation Mastery" Course, $497 © 8 video training modules © Monthly group Q&A calls © Speech review community

- Back-end product 2: "Speaker's Inner Circle" ($997/quarter) © Weekly group coaching © Speech makeover workshops © Network with other speakers

e® Back-end product 3: "VIP Speech Coaching" ($1, 499/month) © 1:1 coaching with Sarah © Custom speech development © Video analysis and feedback

- Back-end product 4: "Corporate Speech Coaching" (Price upon request, $10,000+)
- 2+ days of tailored, in-person training at your facility © Custom speech development

Now that you understand the architecture of The Perfect Funnel, let’s see how you can price your offers at each stage.

After all, your pricing structure is the backbone of your entire funnel.

Get it wrong, and even the best strategy will underperform — and you’ll spin your wheels wondering why...

Get it right, and you’ll maximize both conversions and revenue...

And make your clients - and you - rich in the process.

Pricing

I recommend you keep your pricing structure simple and clear.

Let’s see what our fictional public speaking coach offers...

- Front-end: $6 ebook with 3 bonuses © Upsell: $97 course © Downsell: $37 lighter course © Cross-sell: $199 workshop excerpt © Downsell: $39/mo community


<!-- Source: Day 19 - The Perfect Funnel.pdf; page 14 -->

- Back-end 1: $497 course
- Back-end 2: $997/quarter
- Back-end 3: $1,499/month 1-1 coaching
- Back-end 4: $10,000+ corporate offer

There are other ways. Here’s an example that uses multiple front-end products.

- Front-end 1: $29 report
- Front-end 2: $9.87 book
- Front-end 3: $97 workshop
- Back-end 1: $197 course
- Back-end 2: $997/year or $97/month mastermind
- Back-end 3: $1,997/month 1-1 coaching

Here’s another that starts out with a front-end subscription offer:

- Front end 1: $29/year subscription
- Back end 1: $129 course
- Back end 2: $2,997 course
- Back end 3: $16,999/year mastermind

These are just examples and depending on your situation, they may or may not apply.

All I’m asking you to consider is the clarity of your offers.

They should have a clear logic to them, which can be:

1. Do It Yourself offers for Acquisition and Conversion.
2. Done With You offers for Conversion and Monetization.
3. Done For You offers for Monetization.

There are also other models to consider:

- You can have a ‘descending’ offer structure, where you begin with your most expensive offer, and sell it through a webinar, for instance. You can test this simultaneously to diversify your Acquisition.

- You can use an ‘offer bubble’ structure, where you have one product, but offer different levels of access: © Pay $219/week and get all the training material © Paying $799/month and get everything above + live group calls © Paying $2499/3 months and get everything above + 1-1 coaching


<!-- Source: Day 19 - The Perfect Funnel.pdf; page 15 -->

This structure is great when you don’t have the time (or the willingness) to create multiple products.

Whatever you choose, remember this: Segmentation is crucial, because you do not talk to different people the same way.

Non-buyers cannot get access to back-end offers, even if it’s tempting.

This will not work as well as you think it will. The only thing it’ll do is confuse your audience.

The Perfect Funnel isn't just about making sales - it's about building sustainable systems so your business serves both you and your customers.

By separating your funnel into Acquisition, Conversion, and Monetization, you can focus on optimizing each component while maintaining a clear view of the bigger picture.

Now remember: Your success doesn't depend on getting everything perfect from day one.

Start with one solid front-end offer, nail your acquisition strategy, then gradually build out your back-end.

Test, measure, and adjust as you go.

Now that you understand this architecture, you’ll be able to not only write better Copy, but to help your clients build better businesses.

And that's what will separate you from the crowd and justify charging premium rates.

Now check out the next page for a fictional, yet realistic, example of The Perfect Funnel applied to physical products.


<!-- Source: Day 19 - The Perfect Funnel.pdf; page 16 -->

The Perfect Funnel For Physical Products

Let’s say you run a fitness brand focused on bodyweight training.

You sell pull up bars, gymnastics rings, parallettes (small, portable push-up bars), resistance bands, etc.

Your marketing is around the idea of ‘train anywhere, anytime.’ ‘You don’t need a gym to build muscle.’ ‘Less equipment, more freedom.’

Here’s how you could structure your Perfect Funnel:

- Lead Magnet: “The Minimalist’s Guide To Bodyweight Mastery”

- Front-end product at $9.95: Single Resistance Band + 30-day mobility routine + “Movement Mastery” video library + travel workout guide + recovery guide

© Upsell at $77: "Mobility Master Kit" that includes a full set of resistance band + Door anchor + 90-day mobility program © Downsell at $37: "Mobility Starter Kit" - Light and medium bands + Basic mobility guide © Cross-sell at $147: Wooden Gymnastics Rings + rings training program © Cross-sell downsell at $57: Portable parallettes + parallettes training program

- Back-end product 1 as a $39/mo subscription: “Movement Evolution” © A new program every month © Technique analysis © Live mobility classes weekly © Community challenges © Equipment discounts

- Back-end product 2 at $197: “Movement Fundamentals Kit” © Doorway pull up bar © Portable parallettes © Travel bag with full set of resistance bands © Progressive Skills Program
- Form check through app


<!-- Source: Day 19 - The Perfect Funnel.pdf; page 17 -->

- Back-end product 3 at $497: “Movement Master Bundle” © Premium Wooden Gymnastics Rings
- Elevated parallettes © Travel bag for rings and parallettes © Progressive Skills Program
- Form check through app

- Back-end product 4 at : “Elite Movement Academy”
- 5-day seminar around advanced bodyweight strength moves
- Free gym bag with goodies (t-shirt, wrist wraps, protein wraps, etc)


<!-- Source: Day 19 - The Perfect Funnel.pdf; page 18 -->

### Summary

The Perfect Funnel has 3 components:

1. Acquisition to get new leads and / or customers
- Goal: Break even or profit on initial contact
- Methods: Paid (ads, affiliates) or Organic (content, social media)
- Key metric: Customer Acquisition Cost (CAC)

2. Conversion for first-time buyers
- Goal: Get the first sale
- Focus: Abundance & irresistible offers
- Strategy: Low-ticket + valuable bonuses

3. Monetization for follow-up sales
- Goal: Generate actual profit
- Focus: Certainty & credible promises
- Strategy: Higher-priced back-end offersThere are 3 customer segments:

1. Non-buyers: Lead magnet subscribers
2. Buyers: Have purchased your front-end offer and potentially the first back-end
3. Best Buyers: Have bought multiple back-end offers
- Fanatics who are emotionally invested
- Pure Doers who are focused on value

A winning product structure:

- Front-end:
- Low-ticket product (<$100)
- First upsell (10-20x low ticket) © Downsell © Cross-sell © Downsell to the cross-sell

- Back-end: © Multiple price points
- Ascending value ladder © Focus on transformation © Premium access / coaching


<!-- Source: Day 19 - The Perfect Funnel.pdf; page 19 -->

Pricing philosophy:

- Clear, logical structure
- Do It Yourself + Done With You s Done For You
- Front-end: Focus on conversion
- Back-end: Focus on value delivery
- Strict progression: No back-end offers to non-buyers!

Success formula:

AOV OF THE PRICE OF PROFIT PER CUSTOMER = CAC — —|UPSELL, DOWNSELLS THE PRODUCT & CROSS SELLS

Key principles:

- Start simple, scale gradually
- Have multiple acquisition channels running simultaneously (at least one paid + one organic and build from there)
- Lead magnets solve one problem and create another
- Front-end converts, back-end profits
- Keep content digestible and actionable
- Never ‘break’ your segmentation: No back-end offers to non-buyers!


<!-- Source: Day 19 - The Perfect Funnel.pdf; page 20 -->

### Coming Up Tomorrow...

Clueless Copywriters try to sound clever by blindly parroting their favorite Crocs-wearing bodybuilder turned marketer...

“..Give away everything for free to create goodwill with your audience...”

Without considering that his business has NOTHING to do with theirs.. Or that he’s made his money in a totally different way.

While this approach works for him and his wife, it inevitably leads to the same result for others: Lots of followers but no revenue... Which is fine if you want to be an influencer, by the way...

..But if you were planning on running an actual business that makes a profit, this will have you closer to running a charity instead.

Because here’s the issue: Giving away too much free stuff and never asking for the sale only attracts the worst kind of people: The ‘Triers.’

They’re similar to the ‘Non-Buyers’ you’ve learned about today, but worse. These guys are only here to drain us off our energy, time, and dare I say, willingness to live.

They’re the rookies stuck in tutorial hell, constantly looking for the next shiny object, addicted to ‘shortcuts,’ ‘hacks,’ and avid readers of X threads like ‘The Best Side-Hustles To Reach 10k/mo in 2024...’

They’ll NEVER spend a dime on your products.

So tomorrow, I’ll be showing you how you can REPEL these people WHILE you attract the best kind: the ‘Buyers...’

Thanks to a carefully crafted Welcome Sequence, which is the first series of emails your subscribers will receive.

You’ll discover the ‘OFFER-WHY-WHAT-HOW Model’ that turns lukewarm leads into eager buyers in 4 emails or less..

..The counterintuitive reason you should ‘sell hard & fast’ in your very first email...


<!-- Source: Day 19 - The Perfect Funnel.pdf; page 21 -->

And a simple segmentation trick that can triple your conversions without having to write endless email funnels.

I’ll teach you how to turn freeloaders into buyers with a single, perfectly-timed email...

How to ethically ‘nag’ your subscribers into either taking action... Or unsubscribing...

And how to get the ‘Benefits vs. Features’ balance right every single time.

The knowledge you’re about to receive will let you bring actual results - a.k.a. $$$ - to your clients...

And separate you from clueless marketers who mumble technobabble like ‘impressions’ and ‘views’ when their client asks about their campaign ROI.

Stay tuned.

Your friend,

[Handwritten signature]

## Day 20: The Welcome Sequence

<!-- Source: Day 20 - The Welcome Sequence.pdf; page 1 -->

The Welcome Sequence - also known as the Indoctrination Sequence - is the first set of emails your leads will receive after subscribing to your email list.

As with everything, you’ll need to know the full context to create a working Welcome Sequence.

- WHO?

Who will receive it?

Are they free leads who have just downloaded your lead magnet?

Or are they buying customers who have bought your front-end OR back-end product?

Depending on where they come from, you will have to talk to them differently.

- WHY?

What is your purpose with this Welcome Sequence?

You must have one specific goal, and usually it’s making sales.

But what type of sale? First time sale? Second time sale? Subscription or one-time offer?

- WHAT?

What product will you sell exactly?

It can’t be multiple products. Each one of your Welcome Sequences must have a clear starting point and a clear destination.

You can only know what to do when you know what you want to achieve.

That’s why today we are going to explore a few different scenarios.

But first, let’s talk about the basics of Welcome Sequences.


<!-- Source: Day 20 - The Welcome Sequence.pdf; page 2 -->

The #1 Goal of the Welcome Sequence

You write Welcome Sequences to people who’ve just taken the next step in their customer journey.

For instance:

- Free Leads to First-Time Customers
- First-Time Customers to Recurring Customers
- Recurring Customers to Best Customers

It’s a transformation, a level up.

And so each one needs to have its own introduction, which of course serves the purpose of making them level up again.

That’s why the #1 purpose of the Welcome Sequence is to SELL the next level.

eo) s a” ——< z ra “ . > M4 ele . <— Welcome Sequence 3 ve toe a3 ee ° a § eo? —— os eo ed ° a 4 > <— Welcome Sequence 2 py cd ° s ¢ ——e o€f ° ° zp ° a ° <— Welcome Sequence 1 é ° —

Time

Disclaimer: This graph is for illustrative purposes. I’m not telling you to implement 3+ Welcome Sequences into your email flow...

I’m telling you 2 things:

1. A Welcome Sequence takes your customer further into his journey with you.
2. You can have 2+ Welcome Sequences - if it fits your business.


<!-- Source: Day 20 - The Welcome Sequence.pdf; page 3 -->

Here’s the thing...

I’ve seen too many businesses led by clueless Copywriters who think Welcome Sequences are just about introducing yourself to new potential customers.

I’ve even seen million-dollar publishing companies start with free content (to ‘build up value’) before they dare to send an offer.

This is NOT how you do it. Countless tests have shown that you should be selling right away.

Do you know why?

Because each ‘level’ has its very specific goal. And its very specific way of achieving it.

At the time, giving away value before selling anything felt like a good idea to clueless marketers. They thought that people would hate them less if they did so.

But in reality, they were only satisfying their own false sense of morality...

~Because they were ashamed of selling their products.

Sometimes, they were even ashamed of selling - plain and simple.

In 99% of funnels, you evolve through sales. This is why you must be selling right away...

The Lead to Customer Model

The Lead to Customer Welcome Sequence is the most common case you’ll encounter in your Copywriting journey.

It’s actually what people refer to when they think of a Welcome Sequence. They don’t know that there are in fact many other cases.

Here you start with a free lead that you've acquired - most of the time - through a lead magnet.

They’ve clicked on an ad and joined your email list to receive a bonus video or report.


<!-- Source: Day 20 - The Welcome Sequence.pdf; page 4 -->

Now these people are subscribed to your list, but in truth..

...Most of them don’t want to be here. In fact, they’ll most likely never open your emails, or they’ll unsubscribe when they can.

Here’s your context.

So now you know where you want to take them: You want to sell them your very first front-end offer.

You know what I’m talking about: the ‘Abundance’ offer, where you give a lot of things for a low price.

Remember this, because it will be the spirit you must uphold all throughout the Sequence. It’s about ABUNDANCE, gaining things through a ‘so-fair-it’s-unfair’ deal.

Each one of your emails must be benefit-driven and transformation-oriented.

‘Promise’ rather than ‘Education.’

Now the Welcome Sequence almost writes itself. You have to tempt them to get their credit card out for the very first time.

Remember this: there is no winning template with Welcome Sequences.

You can follow a template, but there’s no guarantee as to whether it’ll work or not.

The best thing you can do is to analyze your specific situation and craft a Welcome Sequence from scratch.

But don’t worry, I’m not going to leave you hanging like that...

~Here’s a logical progression in 4 emails you can follow to craft killer Welcome Sequences.

For the first one...

Email #1

-Waste no time: Make your best promise and offer your product right away.


<!-- Source: Day 20 - The Welcome Sequence.pdf; page 5 -->

This email is crucial because it’s the first one they receive after downloading your lead magnet. It can even be on the thank you page, after they download the lead magnet. It can even be inside the lead magnet itself.

This is when their attention is at 100%. They are primed to buy. This moment will NEVER happen again so don’t miss your shot.

Confront them directly with a huge promise you can deliver on, and an offer so beneficial they would feel dumb to say no to.

Identify their #1 problem and offer a direct solution.

Focus on the benefits and the underlying transformation.

Here you have my permission to go as loud as you can. Be obnoxiously confident you have what they need.

As I said, it’s your best shot, so don’t be shy and don’t make it too ‘quiet.’

The purpose of Email #1 is to cast a wide net that will catch all the people in an ‘Action State.’

It should be your best performing email by far.

Here’s an example for our fictional public speaking coach from the last lesson. Let’s call her Sarah Angelier.

Your new subscriber has just downloaded your lead magnet: “The 5-Min Confidence Boost: Overcome Public Speaking Anxiety With This Short Pre-Speech Ritual...”

And he receives the first email of your Welcome Sequence as a result:

Subject Line: Your confidence boost ritual (and the next step to commanding any room)

Hey [First Name],

Your ‘5-Min Confidence Boost’ ritual is on its way to your inbox right now.

And it’ll change the way you feel about speaking in front of people - forever.


<!-- Source: Day 20 - The Welcome Sequence.pdf; page 6 -->

‘Stage fright’ will become a thing of the past...

...And you’ll finally be able to let your true self shine through, so you can command any room you walk into.

I can’t wait for you to experience this newfound confidence.

But before you dive in, I need to tell you something crucial about being a great public speaker...

That pre-speech ritual? It's just the first piece of the puzzle.

Because here's the truth: Confidence gets you on stage, but it's your PRESENCE that keeps all eyes on you.

And I've discovered that captivating an audience comes down to just 3 psychological triggers that separate dull speakers from unforgettable ones.

+ I've packed these triggers (and the exact scripts to activate them) into my best-selling book "Spellbound: The 60-Page Guide To Captivating Your Audience."

Inside, you'll discover:

- The ‘Story Bridge’ technique that instantly connects you with ANY audience
- My ‘Magnetic Moments’ framework for creating presentations that stick
- My 9 favorite ‘Power Phrases’ that make audiences lean in and pay close attention
- And much, MUCH more...

2> It can be yours for only $6! <<

But it doesn’t end there...

Because when you order TODAY, you’ll be receiving the following FREE bonuses:

- Bonus N°1: “The Full 5-Min Pre-Speech Ritual Follow-Along Video”


<!-- Source: Day 20 - The Welcome Sequence.pdf; page 7 -->

Sarah Angelier

I’m not saying it’s perfect, but it’ll certainly do the job.

Now for Email #2...

Email #2

...Focus on the ‘WHY.’

This one is for the people who have either not read your first email - which means it wasn’t eye-catching enough...

Or the people who aren’t sure they want to buy your solution.

If they’re not sure, they’re still stuck in a problem state...


<!-- Source: Day 20 - The Welcome Sequence.pdf; page 8 -->

...So you need to find out why they’re not sure.

Here are the 3 most common objections your reader may have...

1. “It’s not for me”

They simply don’t believe your solution applies to them, or their specific situation.

2. “I don’t understand what it does”

This means that you haven’t been clear enough on what your product actually does, and what they can expect from it.

This happens a lot when you start writing Copy, because you tend to focus on the extraneous benefits instead of the features...

...Which is one big lie you’ve been told too many times.

You’ve probably seen clueless marketers repost the same generic examples:

Don’t say ‘5GB of storage...’

Say ‘1,000 songs in your pocket’

My brother in Christ. You’re not Apple. And you - and your client - are not a billion dollar company. And your market is different. And your product is not the iPod..

You get my point.

When you focus only on extraneous benefits, your offer will be too vague. When you’re looking to buy a car, for instance, you don’t expect the salesman to only tell you how ‘amazing’ the driving experience will be...

You still want to know the engine size, mileage, or even how many doors it has.

Features are important, yet too many beginner Copywriters skip them altogether.

Here’s an easy model you can follow:

“[Product] does [benefit] thanks to [feature].”


<!-- Source: Day 20 - The Welcome Sequence.pdf; page 9 -->

Remember: You have just about a second to clarify what your product actually does.

But if your reader is drowning in abstract benefits, there’s no way he’ll understand what your product does.

3. “I’m not sure it’ll work”

This means that your prospect doesn’t believe it’ll bring them the results they expect.

You need to show them - through case studies and testimonials
- that it’s exactly what they need.

Don’t be afraid of making your product SPECIFIC to certain people.

The simplest way to do this is to duplicate your Acquisition funnel and create ‘sub-versions,’ like so...

- Original Funnel: How to Captivate Any Audience With Public Speaking
- Duplicata 1: How Introverts Can Captivate Any Audience With Public Speaking
- Duplicata 2: How IT Professionals Can Captivate Any Audience With Public Speaking
- Duplicata 3: How Non-Native English Speakers Can Captivate Any Audience With Public Speaking
- Etc...

You can have many little funnels running alongside each other, where 90%+ of the content is the same, but the headlines, titles, Unique Mechanisms, etc, are made ultra-specific.

For instance, in our public speaking example, Sarah Angelier could take her ‘Spellbound’ guide and create versions specifically targeting different audience segments.

The core psychological triggers would remain the same, but the examples, case studies, and specific applications would be tailored to each group.

You would then run a specific Welcome Sequence for each, according to your segmentation.

Back to email #2..


<!-- Source: Day 20 - The Welcome Sequence.pdf; page 10 -->

This one is all about the ‘Why.’

Why you’re writing them an email. Why ‘something big’ is happening. Why it will transform their life.

And why they should buy right now.

The idea is to put words on the context and the situation.

Most of the time, it revolves around a specific problem.

Going back to our public speaking example, it's about professionals who have mastered basic confidence but still can't truly connect with their audience.

It's also about showing your readers that mastering psychological triggers - not just surface-level techniques - is the real key to memorable presentations.

You're making a diagnosis. You need to describe the problem before you can solve it.

This is exactly what Sarah does in her second email when she talks about speakers who seem confident but still lose the room.

She's diagnosing the gap between confidence and true audience connection.

And this is the very purpose of email #2.

But once again, don’t spend too much time on educating your readers...

Focus on making them salivate.

Here’s how Sarah Angelier could go about it..

Hey [First Name],

Standing tall and proud, with strong eye contact, and an assertive voice...


<!-- Source: Day 20 - The Welcome Sequence.pdf; page 11 -->

Professionals master the basics of confidence (like the ritual I shared with you), but still struggle to truly CONNECT with their audience.

Next, with email #3...

Email #3

You want to focus on the ‘WHAT.’

This is the ‘meat’ of the problem. Now that they know they have a problem, it’s about leading them towards taking action.

But people can’t take action if they don’t know what the action is...

...Which is why Email #3 needs to be as clear as possible about the opportunity you’re giving them.

Going back to our public speaking example, here Sarah would show them what they'll be able to achieve thanks to the psychological triggers in ‘Spellbound.’


<!-- Source: Day 20 - The Welcome Sequence.pdf; page 12 -->

She would paint the picture of giving presentations that not only look confident but actually move people to action.

This is a 'solution-oriented' email, rather than a diagnosis.

Just like your doctor explains how the medication works in your body, Sarah explains how the psychological triggers work in the audience's mind.

Like so..

In Email #3 you compare your solution to others your lead knows about, or has tried unsuccessfully in the past.

It doesn’t have to be long!

This will allow you to talk about your Unique Mechanism. You will highlight the logical difference that makes your solution unique and effective.

Once again, focus on the consequences of the mechanism, the underlying benefit, its uniqueness, effectiveness, and difference.

Don’t overeducate them or go into too much unnecessary detail.

Email #4

Email #4 is about the ‘HOW.’

Here we go back to the offer presented in Email #1. You will be addressing the people who are ready to take action because they believe in the opportunity you’ve shown them.

Here you want to be as precise as possible about what’s going to happen to them when they take you up on your offer.

Be as concrete as possible, go through each step they will take...

- First you will click on this button...
- You will then land on a secured order form...
- You will be able to download [Product Name]...
- Etc

Lay things out clearly for them, so they don’t have any unpleasant surprises.

Also tell them exactly what they’re going to get, in detail.

For instance, if you’re selling a live workshop or webinar, address all questions - even the ones you think are obvious.


<!-- Source: Day 20 - The Welcome Sequence.pdf; page 14 -->

- Will they be able to ask questions?
- How can they ask questions?
- Will they be able to keep the recording?
- Etc

It’s about making the path ahead chartered: As clear as possible...

~So that the ultimate step is about getting their card out of their wallet.

Email #5, #6, and Beyond

Every following email of your Welcome Sequence is about nagging them.

It might sound ‘bad’, but it’s true.

Your free leads have to remember that you’re not a charity or an entertainer.

Your readers are here for a reason: To get the results they want thanks to your products and services.

Nagging them means making them aware that they’ re wasting everyone’s time, and that they should either unsubscribe or buy your no-brainer product.

In reality, 90% of free leads will never buy a single product from you. Remember the ‘Non-Buyers’ from yesterday’s lesson.

That means you have a bunch of freeloaders mindlessly consuming your free content.

You don’t want those people around.

Your goal is to make them leave, or make them buy.


<!-- Source: Day 20 - The Welcome Sequence.pdf; page 16 -->

You can have several emails following your core 4 emails:

- Testimonials
- Case-studies
- Guarantee
- FOMO and scarcity based emails
- And even a deadline-before-deletion email

The idea is to understand which step of the ‘WHY-WHAT-HOW’ awareness process they’re stuck in.

You do this by analyzing the results of each email.

If your ‘WHY’ emails don’t work, that may be because they’ re past that stage already.

If your ‘WHAT’ emails don’t work, it’s either because they’ re stuck in the WHY, or past that stage.

If your ‘HOW’ emails don’t work, it’s because they’ re not convinced by your offer.

It’s not easy to know exactly why people aren’t buying. And any guess remains just a guess.

But I can assure you that going back to a simple-to-analyze Welcome Sequence, such as the one I’ve given you above, solves most of these issues, and prevents you from having to conduct deep analysis to figure out why people aren’t buying.

The Other Types of Welcome Sequences

When it comes to all the other types of Welcome Sequences, the model remains the same, but the spirit isn’t.

With First-Time Customer to Second-Time Customer...

You’re talking to a specific section of your First-Time Customers.

Those with the buying power, and the specific issues, that you're targeting.

As I’ve said before, Front-End means ‘Abundance’, whereas Back-End means ‘Certainty.’

This means that you have to follow the same WHY-WHAT-HOW logical sequence, but with results in mind.


<!-- Source: Day 20 - The Welcome Sequence.pdf; page 18 -->

Here it’s more about proving to them your solution works, and that it comes with no risk.

It’s also about making it as customized as possible. And I’m not talking about my previous Youtube example. I’m talking about an almost Bespoke offer, tailored to your specific customer.

For instance, Sarah's $6 ‘Spellbound’ guide is a Front-End offer packed with abundance - the guide itself plus 3 valuable bonuses.

But her Back-End offer is her ‘Presentation Mastery’ Course priced at $497. With 8 video training modules, monthly group Q&A calls, and a speech review community, the student is guaranteed to improve his public speaking skills through more tailored content and feedback.

Then, she could be offering her ‘VIP Speech Coaching’ priced at $1,499 per month. This offer guarantees to transform someone into a compelling speaker through highly-personalized guidance.

This means that you have to follow the same WHY-WHAT-HOW logical sequence, but with results in mind.

Sarah would focus less on the problem of public speaking anxiety (which she addressed in the Front-End) and more on proven results from her personalized coaching approach.

This is why 1-1 offers work so well, because it’s exclusive and specific to their situation. This means the chance of it bringing results is much higher.

In fact, it’s almost certain.

When analyzed side by side, the first Welcome Sequence I’ve shown you and this one are not similar, yet they follow the same underlying logic.

If you’re curious to see how Sarah could go about ‘leveling up’ a first-time customer, I’ll add that welcome sequence at the end of lesson.

There’s one last point I’d like to cover...


<!-- Source: Day 20 - The Welcome Sequence.pdf; page 19 -->

What about storytelling?

You’ve probably heard about a type of Welcome Sequence where it’s all about telling your own rags-to-riches story.

If you have one, then feel free to tell it because it does follow the WHY-WHAT-HOW logical structure.

But some people don’t have one, and that’s why I didn’t include it as a requirement.

With my model it’s not exactly about how you write your emails...

...It’s about the process and the spirit.

As long as you follow these guidelines, feel free to write your emails exactly like you want, and according to your specific context.

“NS Summary

A Welcome Sequence is the first set of emails leads receive after subscribing to your list.

Your #1 goal is to sell them to the next level in the customer journey.

Different sequences exist for different stages:

- Leads ~ First-Time Customers
- First-Time Customers — Recurring Customers
- Recurring Customers = Best Customers

The OFFER-WHY-WHAT-HOW Framework:

- Email #1: The Offer
- Make your best promise immediately
- Present your offer when attention is at 100% © Focus on benefits and transformation © Be confident & direct

- Email #2: The WHY © Address the most common objections:
- "It's not for me" m "I don't understand what it does"
- "I'm not sure it'll work"
- Explain the context
- Diagnose the problem

- Email #3: The WHAT © Focus on the solution © Highlight your Unique Mechanism © Compare your solution to alternatives © Explain how it works without over-educating

- Email #4: The HOW
- Explain the process ahead © Detail the exact steps they’ll take
- List everything they’ll receive © Address common questions
- Include guarantee details


<!-- Source: Day 20 - The Welcome Sequence.pdf; page 28 -->

- Emails #5 and beyond: © Focus on: m Testimonials ™ Case studies m Guarantees ™ FOMO and scarcity m Deadline reminders

Key principles:

1. Sell immediately: Don't delay with free content

2.Each level has specific goals and approaches: a. Front-End: Focus on ‘abundance’ b. Back-End: Focus on ‘certainty’

3. Customize your approach based on: a. Who will receive it b. What you want to achieve c. What specific product you're selling

4. Keep analyzing results to understand where prospects are getting stuck in the OFFER-WHY-WHAT-HOW process

And remember:

- Welcome Sequences should be tailored to your specific context
- No winning template works for everyone
- Focus on the process and spirit rather than rigid rules
- Storytelling can be incorporated if relevant but isn't required

## Day 21: The Presale Sequence

<!-- Source: Day 21 - The Presale Sequence (2).pdf; page 1 -->

A few years ago, Direct Response giant QM bit a wall.

Sales slowed.

It was the end of a cycle. Some even called it...

..The end of an era.

But something unexpected happened.

A Copywriter tried a new system, which made them blast through their plateau and reach entirely new heights.

The breakthrough he found?

The Presale Sequence.

And it works so well that I never write a sales page or VSL without it anymore!

In fact, a Presale Sequence will multiply your sales by 2x or 3x alone.

And it’s easy to do and implement.

It works by simply sending 4-5 emails before launching a sales page or VSL.

The concept was first introduced by Direct Response marketing genius Jeff Walker, who called it the Product-Launch Formula (PLF.)

To pay homage to his work, we’ll call it the PLF from now on.

The PLF

With that said, I’m not going to regurgitate Jeff Walker’s book, which you can read.

I’m going to share my own version of the PLF instead...

..The one that I’ve tweaked for years to make it as powerful as possible.


<!-- Source: Day 21 - The Presale Sequence (2).pdf; page 2 -->

I typically write 4 emails, although in certain circumstances I’ll add a last one (I’ll tell you why in a minute.)

They’ re called:

1. The PPLC, which stands for Pre-Product-Launch Content.
2. The PLC1, which stands for Product-Launch Content 1.
3. The PLC2
4. The PLC3
5. The PLC4 (Sometimes.)

Each email serves a distinct purpose.

PPLC

The PPLC acts as a teaser and as a ‘triage’ email. It doesn’t sell anything yet.

It’s the introduction to the problem, the Big Idea, and the solution. It’s also the beginning of the story.

The role of this email is to make people excited to read the rest. Because at the end of it, you’re going to insert a link so they can opt-in a special sequence of emails.

But be careful, do not solely focus on making the PPLC intriguing!

Tie it to a concrete promise and tease a solution.

People should opt-in for the solution, not only the story.

This will ensure that you have qualified clicks and engaged readers.

Once they click to opt-in the sequence, you’ll be able to tag them, which will allow you to target them specifically.

Can you do it without an opt-in?

Yes, but your numbers will be vague and hard to read.

If your list is too small then you don’t need an opt-in link.

In that case, you simply turn your PPLC into a classic teaser, with no link at the end.


<!-- Source: Day 21 - The Presale Sequence (2).pdf; page 3 -->

Do you remember Sarah Angelier, our public speaking coach from last lesson?

Good.

If she were to write a presale sequence for her back-end product, the ‘Presentation Mastery’ course, here’s how she could go about it..

Subject Line: This ‘invisible force’ makes or breaks your presentations

Hey [First Name],

Last week, I watched something fascinating happen at a tech conference.

Two speakers presented what was in essence the exact same product, with nearly identical slides.

Both were confident and well-prepared (I even shared with them my 5-minute ritual before going on stage).

The first speaker got polite applause.

The second received a standing ovation and was swarmed by investors afterward.

The difference?

Something I call ‘Presentation Architecture’ - a hidden framework that turns ordinary speeches into unforgettable experiences.

It's not about confidence (though that helps).

It's not about your slides (though they matter).

It's not even about your speaking skills (though they’re crucial).

Because when you’re pitching the top investors in the country, all of these are taken for granted. All the startup founders have had some form of training when it comes to speaking in public.

I’m talking about something that goes beyond the surface level...


<!-- Source: Day 21 - The Presale Sequence (2).pdf; page 4 -->

I’m talking about the invisible force that makes some presentations resonate deeply while others fall flat.

I've spent the last 3 years decoding this framework by studying hundreds of legendary speakers - from Steve Jobs to Tony Robbins.

And I've discovered something surprising: The best presentations follow a specific emotional ‘architecture’ that you can learn and replicate.

Would you like to know more?

I'll be sharing my complete findings in a special email series next week.

>> Click here to join the waitlist! <<

To your success,

Sarah Angelier

P.S. This isn't theory - it's the same framework that helped my client land a $2M investment after just 12 minutes on stage.

This email creates intrigue with a real-world story and introduces the core concept of ‘Presentation Architecture.’

It’s not only intriguing, it’s tied up to a benefit too.

PLC1

PLC1 is your most important email.

It will make or break your whole sequence.

In the PLCl you must talk about two things:

1. WHY you’re talking to them 2.The ‘Enjeu:’ what’s at stake here

Addressing the ‘WHY’ gives the whole sequence a reason to exist. And tells them to pay attention.

The ‘Enjeu’ is about the problem: What they have to lose and what they have to gain.


<!-- Source: Day 21 - The Presale Sequence (2).pdf; page 5 -->

You state the problem, agitate the emotions, warn them about not taking action, and tease the solution and the best outcome.

If you’re telling a story, that’s also where it starts.

You then conclude your PLC1 with a teaser for PLC2.

No product reveal and no selling yet!

Here’s how Sandra could write hers...

Subject: Why most ‘great speakers’ never truly connect

Hey [First Name],

Remember that tech conference I mentioned?

Here's what happened next: The second speaker (let's call him Michael) reached out to me afterward.

"Sarah," he said, "I've been hiding something from my team. That presentation? It was my first success in 18 months. Before that, I was the guy people politely smiled at - then forgot."

Michael's story hit home because I've seen this pattern hundreds of times:

Talented professionals master the basics of public speaking.. They conquer their fear.. They learn to project confidence... But something's still missing.

They deliver ‘good’ presentations that everyone likes yet nobody remembers.

This matters more than ever because in today's world, being forgettable is almost worse than being bad.

Every presentation is a chance to:

- Advance your career
- Secure that funding
- Rally your team
- Change minds and inspire action

And it’s what keeps me up at night...


<!-- Source: Day 21 - The Presale Sequence (2).pdf; page 6 -->

Most speakers are leaving 80% of their impact on the table.

Tomorrow, I'll show you the framework that changed everything for Michael (and dozens of my other clients).

It's simpler than you might think - but only if you understand the science behind it.

To your success,

Sarah Angelier

PLCl focuses on the ‘WHY:’ The danger of being a speaker no one remembers.

PLC2

PLC2 is the ‘heart’ of your campaign. In this email you’re writing to them about the ‘WHAT.’

What’s going on. What’s happening.

In PLCl you were retaining information, focusing on the problem.

Now you’re addressing the mechanism of the problem.

At the end of PLC2 your reader must know exactly why they’ve encountered their problem, and what the mechanism of the solution is.

The only thing remaining is the actual solution, and its name.

So that's what you tease at the end of PLC2 (still no reveal or selling at this point.)

Here’s Sarah’s PLC2..

Subject Line: The science of unforgettable presentations

Hey [First Name],

What makes a presentation unforgettable?

After analyzing hundreds of successful speeches, I found something surprising:


<!-- Source: Day 21 - The Presale Sequence (2).pdf; page 7 -->

It's not talent. It’s not confidence. It's not even charisma.

The most memorable presentations follow a precise emotional architecture that triggers 3 specific responses in the human brain:

1. The ‘Peak-End Effect’ - Your brain doesn't remember entire experiences. It only remembers peaks of emotion and how things end.
2. The ‘Story Mirror’ - When a speaker and audience are truly connected, their brain patterns actually synchronize (yes, there's research on this!)
3. The ‘Action Catalyst’ - There's a specific moment in every great presentation where emotion transforms into inevitable action.

Most speakers focus on WHAT they're saying...

Great speakers engineer HOW it's received.

This is why some presentations fall flat while others move mountains.

Tomorrow, I'm going to show you how to put this knowledge into action - and how some of my clients are using it to transform their careers.

To your success,

Sarah Angelier

PLC2 reveals the scientific mechanism behind memorable presentations - the ‘WHAT.’

PLC3

Now for PLC3, we address the ‘HOW.’

In this email you finally reveal the solution.

You explain its origin.

Its benefits.

And how it leads to the desired outcome.


<!-- Source: Day 21 - The Presale Sequence (2).pdf; page 8 -->

You are showing them an easy solution and telling them how to access it.

Here’s how Sarah’s email could look like...

Subject Line: Your bridge to presentation mastery

Hey [First Name],

Over the past few days, I've shown you why most presentations fall flat - even when delivered by confident speakers.

I've also revealed the science behind unforgettable presentations.

Now, I'm thrilled to introduce you to something I've developed over 3 years of research and testing:

The ‘Presentation Mastery’ Program.

This comprehensive system includes:

- The Advanced 'Story Architecture' System that show you how to build presentations that hit emotional peaks exactly when you want them to
- The 'Presence Amplifier' Method, or how to command any room without saying a word
- The ‘Influence Cascade' that lets you turn every presentation into a catalyst for action

But this isn't just another online course...

Tomorrow, I'll share all the details about how you can join our community of serious speakers and transform your presentation impact forever.

If you’ve ever dreamt of joining the ranks of the best speakers in the world...

You don’t want to miss it.

To your success,

Sarah Angelier

Here she reveals the solution and its components.


<!-- Source: Day 21 - The Presale Sequence (2).pdf; page 9 -->

PLC3 can end with a link that goes directly to your landing page, effectively ‘launching’ your product on that day.

That’s what you’ll be doing 80% of the time.

With some products and in some niches though, you’ll need a Sth email...

PLC4

PLC4 is the perfect transition from the (abstract) solution to your actual product.

I use it mostly with supplements, or with more complex topics which need further elaborating.

With supplements you usually show them an ingredient in PLC3.

But most formulas contain more than one ingredient.

That’s where PLC4 comes in: To bridge the main ingredient with the actual formula.

At the end of PLC4 you link to your landing page.

In Sarah’s case, she could have inserted a link to her sales page at the end of PLC3.

But for the purposes of the lesson, here’s how PLC4 could look for her ‘Presentation Mastery’ launch...

Subject: The Presentation Mastery Program is now open [Limited Time]

Hey [First Name],

It’s time!

The ‘Presentation Mastery’ program is now open for enrollment...

.And I want YOU to be among the first to transform your presentation impact forever!

But first, let me be crystal clear about what makes this program different:


<!-- Source: Day 21 - The Presale Sequence (2).pdf; page 10 -->

1. It's based on proven neuroscience, not theory.

Your presentations will be engineered for maximum impact using the same principles that helped my client land that $2M investment I told you about.

2. It includes personal feedback and guidance.

This isn't a ‘watch and hope’ program. You'll get direct feedback on your delivery and approach.

3. You'll be part of a community of serious speakers.

Success doesn't happen in isolation. You'll join a select group of professionals committed to mastering this craft.

Here's exactly what you'll get when you join today:

Core Training:

- The Advanced 'Story Architecture’ System
- The 'Presence Amplifier' Method
- The 'Influence Cascade' Framework

All delivered through 8 comprehensive video modules you can access immediately.

Plus these powerful support elements:

- Monthly Q&A group calls where I personally review your progress
- A supportive community of serious speakers who share feedback
- Direct speech reviews to fine-tune your delivery

You’re not only looking at a comprehensive, cutting-edge training program that is unlike anything available today...

You're looking at MONTHS of support and guidance.

>> Click here to join the Presentation Mastery Program <<

To your success,

Sarah Angelier


<!-- Source: Day 21 - The Presale Sequence (2).pdf; page 11 -->

P.S. The first 20 members who join will get a private 30-minute strategy session with me to map out their presentation transformation plan ($200 value)

Don't wait - click here to secure your spot!

This last email bridges to the actual product and emphasizes the supportive ecosystem around the core training.

Here’s an overview of the whole sequence.

OPT-IN . ° ° .

Let NEON ° , ) .

Se ex BAe iy =a Dx] =a

For maximum effect, the PLF must be devoid of any direct selling.

Your PLF is NOT supposed to sell.

Your sales page will take care of that in due time.

The PLF is here to PRE-SELL - meaning to set the context and the ‘Enjeu.’

You can see the PLF as a primer, or a multiplier.


<!-- Source: Day 21 - The Presale Sequence (2).pdf; page 12 -->

In Jeff Walker’s version of the PLF, Jeff talks about interactivity with the audience: Asking questions or feedback at the end of each email and going back to the feedback at the beginning of each new one.

I’ve found this to make no real difference in terms of conversion.

But it does increase the work, as you’ve got to go through each answer, and take them into account, which can be tedious.

But if you want to make this more personal, knock yourself out.

As for the timing of each email, I recommend 1 a day, or 1 every other day.

You can write longer PLFs (I’ve tried 20-day PLFs), but shorter ones work well.

The Presale Sequence From Your Reader’s POV

I’d like to wrap up this lesson with how your reader perceives the Presale Sequence.

PPLC

Your reader receives an intriguing email that hints at a solution to their problem or makes an interesting promise.

This email creates curiosity, but it doesn’t sell anything.

He’s asked to opt-in to learn more, which creates commitment.

You want to trigger CURIOSITY and HOPE.

PLC1

Your reader learns WHY this matters NOW.

He understands what’s at stake if he doesn’t solve the problem.

The scope of the problem becomes clearer. He realizes there’s more to the situation than he thought.


<!-- Source: Day 21 - The Presale Sequence (2).pdf; page 13 -->

You trigger an ‘AHA Moment’ through AWARENESS and CONCERN.

PLC2

Your reader learns WHAT’s really going on.

He receives insight into the mechanism behind his problem. He starts to understand why previous solutions may have failed.

The solution is teased - but not revealed.

You want to trigger ANTICIPATION.

PLC3

Your reader learns HOW his problem can be solved.

The solution is finally revealed! He understands the path forward.

You trigger RELIEF and EXCITEMENT.

PLC4

Your reader sees how the solution connects to a specific product.

He understands the practical implementation.

This email triggers ACTION.

NOTE: You can merge PLC3 and PLC4 if your solution is simple and straightforward.

You want to use both when the solution is more complex.

The PLF is one of the most powerful tools in modern DR.

It allows you to double or triple your sales by building anticipation and addressing your reader’s needs, questions and objections BEFORE he sees your sales page.

Because the goal isn’t to sell in your emails...

It’s to create the perfect environment for your sales message, so that when he reaches your sales page, he’s already pre-sold on your solution.

Think of the PLF as laying down the red carpet for your offer.


<!-- Source: Day 21 - The Presale Sequence (2).pdf; page 14 -->

### Summary

The Presale Sequence is a series of 4-5 emails sent before launching a sales page or VSL.

Each email serves a specific purpose:

- PPLC: Teases the solution and creates initial curiosity
- PLC1: Establishes WHY this matters and what's at stake
- PLC2: Explains WHAT's really happening (the mechanism)
- PLC3: Reveals HOW the solution works
- PLC4 (Optional:) Bridges the solution to your specific product

Best practices:

- Send 1 email per day or every other day
- Focus on pre-selling, not selling
- Keep the sequence focused and concise
- Always tie intrigue to concrete benefits
- Use an opt-in link in PPLC for better tracking (unless your list is small)

The sequence works by systematically building:

1. Curiosity and Hope (PPLC)
2. Awareness and Concern (PLC1)
3. Anticipation (PLC2)
4. Relief and Excitement (PLC3)
5. Action (PLC4)


<!-- Source: Day 21 - The Presale Sequence (2).pdf; page 15 -->

The Liftnote..

Or the ‘Wide Net Technique’ that lets you strike the perfect balance between being ultra-specific and generic...

And 3 dead-simple ways to elude the tone of ‘Trust Me Bro’ that plagues too much of today’s Copy..

You’ll be able to prevent common mistakes that are hard to spot to the untrained eye...

Like ‘insulting your reader’s intelligence...’


<!-- Source: Day 21 - The Presale Sequence (2).pdf; page 16 -->

..Or why most Copywriters get the ‘P.S.’ dead wrong and how to use it to actually boost your sales.

You have a packed lesson waiting for you...

And I’m sure you’ll refer back to it in years to come.

As always - make sure you do the accompanying exercises.

They’ll make all the difference in the long run.

Your friend,
